#6869. Esquire First Look: How Bad Monkey Season 2 Brews Up Even More Trouble in Paradise

Creator and showrunner Bill Lawrence reveals how John Malkovich’s love of the Apple TV crime series starring Vince Vaughn landed him a role as the “comedic and dangerous” new villain.

For Bill Lawrence, producing some of TV’s biggest comedies is serious business. Once the architect of cable juggernauts Scrubs and Cougar Town, the 57-year-old producer is now at the helm of several streaming-era darlings, including Ted Lasso, Shrinking, Rooster, and Bad Monkey. But of all his shows, Lawrence tells Esquire that Bad Monkey scratches a specific itch.

“My favorite movies, books, and TV shows that grabbed me as a kid melted genres,” Lawrence says. A self-professed diehard for buddy-cop classics like 48 Hrs. and Beverly Hills Cop, the two-time Emmy winner says that the films’ signature mixture of humor with grittier themes and settings made them more than just funny movies. “People remember them as raucous comedies, and they were. But there were real emotional stakes,” says Lawrence. “And the first author that got me into that stuff was Carl Hiaasen.”

A political journalist and author from Florida (where Lawrence’s family originates), Carl Hiaasen wrote the satirical crime novel Bad Monkey in 2013. The book was adapted in full in 2024 as the Apple TV series’ first season. Lawrence was, and is, a “massive fan” of Hiaasen’s. “Then I got to know this guy,” he says. “That one of my heroes turned out to be like I hoped he would be, and that he let me take one of his best properties and turn it into a little bit of throwback TV—it’s one of the most fun rides of my career.”

Now Lawrence is ready to unpack the upcoming second season of Bad Monkey—and its Emmy-worthy newcomers—which returns to Apple TV on December 2. A new trailer, launching today, is embedded just below.

Bad Monkey season 2 sees Vince Vaughn returns as Yancy, a former Miami detective turned reluctant restaurant inspector who lives in the Florida Keys. With severed arms and medical fraud deep in his rearview mirror, Yancy finds himself caught in a new case involving a shady restaurant—an obvious front for a criminal enterprise—and the underworld boss behind it. Three-time Emmy Award winner and Oscar nominee John Malkovich joins as a new antagonist, Spencer. While Lawrence resists revealing too much about Spencer, he describes the role as a “comedic and dangerous” villain who drives Yancy to the brink.

“One of my favorite things about Carl’s books is that they’re really, at their core, character stories,” Lawrence says. “Spencer” is a role that Lawrence and his team “reverse-engineered” for Malkovich when they caught wind of the actor praising Bad Monkey while promoting his 2025 film Opus. “Randomly, Malkovich said, ‘My favorite show this year was Bad Monkey,’ ” Lawrence recalls. “This story started from a place of [thinking], If John Malkovich was kind and silly enough to bring him in this world, who would he be? You will meet him as our other fearless leader this year.”

The second season of Bad Monkey breaks from Hiaasen’s books (with the author’s blessing) to tell a new story that, in a spiritual sense, takes place between the original Bad Monkey novel and its 2016 sequel, Razor Girl. “We took massive liberties expanding certain characters, making the world bigger,” Lawrence says about collaborating with Hiaasen. “My nightmare would be to do anything he didn’t feel was true to Florida or his voice. I’m not overstepping when I tell you he helped come up with the main issue that Rosa’s chasing down [in season 2], because it was something he had written about when he was a journalist.”

Adds Lawrence, “It was nerve-racking. One of the scariest things, if you’re doing an IP [series] and it’s your favorite author, [is when you] have to make up things, but I’m very lucky he’s become a close friend and is candid with me to tell me if we hit or miss on it.”

Malkovich isn’t the only new face in season 2. A slew of stand-up comics, up-and-coming actors, and notable names fill out the ensemble. Sam Jaeger, Zavior Phillips, Peter Billingsley, Sofia Boutella, and Mo Amer all appear in the second season, to name a few. The Handmaid’s Tale’s Yvonne Strahovski also joins as a series regular in a role Lawrence hints has “something much bigger and deeper going on.” Returning characters from season 1 include Rosa (Natalie Martinez), Ro (John Ortiz), and Caitlin (Charlotte Lawrence, Bill’s real-life daughter).

But Bad Monkey is still all about Vince Vaughn’s Yancy, the endlessly witty, if also damaged, soul of the whole shebang. Yancy has changed a little since season 1—“He realized that he was a catalyst for change in other people,” Lawrence says—but he’s also the same dude who knows how to relax in his own backyard. “He was still drifting around and ended up very alone. Were we to have him be the same loner throughout season 2, it would be a mistake. He is the same Yancy. But watching that character decide what it means to let people into his life—whether it be Caitlin or Rosa—that is his personal journey. I wouldn’t want to watch the show if it weren’t Yancy being Yancy on some level.”

Yancy’s big story for season 2 involves his father, Jim, played again by Scott Glenn, whom Yancy invites to stay with him in his home. Lawrence says this story aims to highlight Yancy’s internal struggle as a budding father figure. “We all have dynamics with our fathers—me, Vince, [co-executive producer] Matt [Tarses]. You become your parent’s ... I don’t want to say caretaker, but the roles switch. That’s a role that comes your way. The person who always could take care of you, you need to step up and do that for them and embrace taking their role for everyone else.”

“If you ask me what [season 2] is actually about,” says Lawrence, “It’s about Yancy stepping in to be the patriarch of whatever his found family is, and whether or not he is ready to do that.”

If one thing sets Bad Monkey apart from Lawrence’s other works, it’s action. Lawrence aims to pay proper homage to the movies that influenced him with “feature-level” set pieces this year. “In season 1, we were flying by the seat of our pants, seeing what we could do, especially in Florida,” says Lawrence. “The one thing we wanted to upgrade in season 2 is the action. It’s stuff I haven’t shot in my normal 30-minute comedies before, and I think we pulled it off.”

The action will be felt through the characters—the ones standing, anyway. Bill Lawrence teases that the sheer amount of newcomers means one or two or several won’t make it to the end. “One of the things about Carl’s books is that when you reach the end, a lot of the regulars have died. Knowing the cast will never get too big, it’s fun to see the smoke clear and [find out] who’s left.”

This is serious business. But it’s still a blast.

#6867. Nicole Kidman Brings Her Youngest Daughter, Faith Margaret, to the Starry Chanel Cruise Show

The pair looked so in sync while showing off their individual styles

When it comes to fashion events, Nicole Kidman tends to brings her eldest biological daughter, Sunday Rose, 18, as her plus-one. This past May, the two walked the red carpet together at the Met Gala in fabulous designer gowns, mere months after Sunday hit the runway for the Dior Spring/Summer 2026 haute couture show. Not to mention, Sunday has also accompanied her mother to events ranging from private Miu Miu parties to the 2024 Paris Summer Olympics.

So, it came as a bit of a surprise when, at the Chanel Cruise 2026-27 show in Sydney, Australia, this morning, Kidman decided instead to bring along her youngest daughter, Faith Margaret Kidman Urban, 15.

At the show, Kidman went classic in a black suit look that was like a deconstructed tuxedo. The ensemble consisted of a cropped black jacket with coattails around back, which she paired with matching straight-leg slacks and layered over contrasting white bases.

Kidman left her jacket open to reveal a thin white vest, which itself was layered over a white tank top. It was a simple, but elegant fit, and the Oscar winner wore a pair of inky satin pumps to round things out.

Faith, meanwhile, looked a bit cozier in a ribbed zip-up jacket in cream, paired with a matching mini skirt. The jacket featured two tiers of gold-studded pockets in front, and it was closed all the way up to its pointy collar. As for accessories, the 15-year-old carried a suede, camel-colored handbag, and she slipped into a pair of strappy silver mules down low.

While in years past Kidman may have been opting to shield her youngest child from the spotlight, Faith has been accompanying her mother to more and more events as of late.

Back in September, the two were seen attending the US Open together, and just last month, Faith sat front row alongside her mom at the Chanel Spring/Summer 2027 ready-to-wear show.

Don’t be surprised if you see more of this high-profile teen at future events.

#6865. Michael J. Fox Says Parkinson's Taught Him This

“I don’t look at any time [in my life] and say, ‘Who was I then?’ or ‘Who will I be in a week?’ or “Who am I now?’ ” the 65-year-old Back to the Future actor says.

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

Michael J. Fox is a fighter. When the Back to the Future and Spin City actor was diagnosed with Parkinson’s disease in 1991, he refused to let the condition cut his life or his career short. He kept acting, earned some Emmy Awards and Golden Globes statuettes, and most recently appeared on season 3 of Apple TV’s Shrinking with Harrison Ford.

As the 65-year-old actor tells Esquire editor in chief Michael Sebastian in the latest episode of “What I’ve Learned,” his drive all stems from a quote his father told him when he left Canada to pursue acting in America: “Well, if you’re going to be a lumberjack, you might as well go to the goddamn forest.”

Though Fox’s father, a former Canadian Armed Forces soldier, was likely talking about Hollywood, Fox says that he took his advice to mean that if you’re going to do anything in life—whether it be his acting, his activism, or even his sheer optimism in the face of disease—you better fully engage yourself in it.

“My parents were born into the Depression ... so they were risk averse. Their whole life was just to have a nice life that didn’t leave anything exposed,” Fox continues. “And I wanted to do all this crazy stuff.” Namely Teen Wolf, three Back to the Future films, playing onstage with Coldplay, poking fun at his condition on Curb Your Enthusiasm (“because it’s hilarious”), and never letting Parkinson’s slow him down.

“I went through eight years of keeping it to myself and trying to forestall it with exercise and diet, learn a little bit from the doctors and experts, and then when I decided to share it it was like this great slap on the forehead,” Fox says about why he formed the Michael J. Fox Foundation. “I thought, There’s all these people that are experiencing this. Why aren’t we sharing our information about it?”

Elsewhere in the episode, Fox shares what he’s learned from Rush Limbaugh accusing him of faking his diagnosis, meeting Barack Obama before he was president, and raising roughly $3 billion for Parkinson’s research. You can watch the episode above, watch it on YouTube here, or listen to it on Spotify and Apple Podcasts.

“I don’t look at any time [in my life] and say, ‘Who was I then?’ or ‘Who will I be in a week?’ or “Who am I now?’ ” Fox says. “I just put my head down enough that I could still see where I was going and just plow ahead. And I realize now that I’m in this great position where I can have an effect on people’s lives.”

#6862. Iowa Governor-Elect Rob Sand Was Also a Prada Model

On November 3, as part of the blue-vote tsunami, Iowa elected Democratic Governor Rob Sand. The 44 year-old graduated with a political science degree from Brown University and a J.D. degree from University of Iowa College of Law; he even got a Truman scholarship and an honors thesis. And his 2026 campaign was centered on anti-corruption, bipartisanship, affordable living, and public education. But there’s more—he’s also a pretty face.

As it turns out, the governor-elect helped pay for his prestigious college degrees by walking runways in Milan and Paris. At six-feet, three inches, the young, aspiring politician modeled in shows for Giuliano Fujiwara and Number (N)ine. He even made it into a Prada runway, Newsweek reports. It was the early 2000s, and he was reportedly signed to none other than IMG Models—though his profile seems to have been taken down since.

For whatever reason, his modeling past is not something the chiseled governor-elect likes to talk about, the New York Times noted, but it sure makes for quite the resume.

On January 19, 2004, Sand even missed the Iowa caucus in Decorah because he was modeling menswear in Milan. “It had to be one or the other,” he told Newsweek. “And I chose to go to Europe.” (Who wouldn’t?) While Sand did not win that time around, look at him now, years later, up at the top.

Sand took Iowa this week while in a sharp navy-blue suit and a sky-blue checkered button-down that matched his eyes. “Thank you, Iowa! I love you, Iowa!” he excitedly said after catwalking up to the podium for his victory speech.

Rob Sand’s team has not responded to Harper’s Bazaar’s request for comment.

#6857. Pastel Hues and Big Jewelry: Decoding The Gilded Age Season 4’s Fashion

The new season of The Gilded Age uses fashion as a powerful storytelling method.

Everyone’s favorite HBO period drama about New York high society at the turn of the 20th century is back for a fourth season. In The Gilded Age, all the main characters (played by Carrie Coon, Christine Baranski, Audra McDonald, Cynthia Nixon, and Morgan Spector) are back. But this time, their outfits are just a little bit different. As old and new money continue to clash, the gowns, jewelry, and glamour speak louder than ever before. The outfits also show where the characters are mentally, more than ever. Within the first episode, we’ve already seen marriage, birth, and daring tensions, all dramatically dressed and detailed.

For the series, costume designer Kasia Walicka-Maimone takes inspiration from the real changing fashion of New York City from the 1870s to the 1890s: a time when trends and silhouettes were rapidly evolving. Walicka-Maimone and her team look to paintings, photography, and fashion plates from the period to inform the grand gowns and refined suiting. “We are seeing more artistic license in the dress designs this season, with traditional elements mixed with modern ones like the sleeveless top Marian wore to the after-wedding party downtown at Webster Hall,” Elizabeth L. Block, author of the Gilded Age Fashion book and newsletter, tells ELLE. “The juxtapositions are fun, maybe even more so than the one-to-one matchups with actual Gilded Age dresses we saw in the earlier seasons.”

Even if we’re early in the season, it’s fascinating to see color tell such a story through clothing. Socialite sisters Agnes van Rhijn (Christine Baranski) and Ada (Cynthia Nixon) are at the center of the show, and in the new season, their looks reflect a slightly more joyful representation of their characters. Think: embroidered details and luscious hues. “Aunt Ada is loosening up,” adds Block. “She is fully out of mourning for her husband, and we are seeing her in lighter color combinations like pink and green. For a while last season she was all buttoned up in black and deep blues.” Gleaming buttons and lace trims allude to the fact that these sisters have access to the best of the best textiles.

On the same wavelength, Peggy (Denée Benton) and her mother, Mrs. Scott (Audra McDonald), demonstrate through their clothing that they have money, but most importantly, taste. There’s a short conversation about Brussels bobbin lace that is quite revealing, however. (Consuelo Vanderbilt famously wore a Brussels lace veil for her glorious 1895 wedding to the Duke of Marlborough.) Their highly ornate outfits with richly detailed trims and embellishments are unrivaled.

Still, sisters Agnes and Ada are more toned down than someone like Bertha Russell (Carrie Coon) and her railroad tycoon husband George Russell (Morgan Spector) or Peggy and her family, who have all always used clothing as armor and a communication tool. Despite seeing Bertha dressed down early in the episode during Gladys’s (Taissa Farmiga) giving birth, she later comes to fruition with diamond tiaras, puffed and beaded sleeves, and embellished full-skirted ball gowns.

A major fashion moment comes when Enid Winterton (Kelley Curran) and Oscar van Rhijn (Blake Ritson) get married. Enid wears an opulent gown embroidered with flowers in a soft shade of pinkish lavender, with matching gloves and an ornate pink floral headpiece to match. As Block says, it’s a “pinky-lavender dress for a lavender marriage,” and “The ensemble immediately reads as incongruous but elegant and playful, like the two characters, who are now fan favorites.” With a flash of pale green and a long train, the look is a nod to the stereotype of the idea of new money being flashier than old money. Enid’s wedding look is the equivalent of a fashion debut for her in a way, and later on, we see her in a pink silk trimmed with sumptuous fur, accented with a pearl necklace and earrings.

We also see Marian Brook (Louisa Jacobson) emerge even more as an example of a young woman who’s interested in dressing differently. Her vivid mint green and black striped gown with draped details was incredibly visually striking and felt new and bold, while her matching hat felt like a nod to the kind of more mature, old-money style of her aunts. She also wears a blue lace gown with velvet shoulder details and matching opera gloves, revealing more skin than the old-money characters in the show.

If you’re a jewelry (or accessories) lover, it’s the season to watch. Enid’s spectacular diamond necklace and whimsical approach to hats covered in bows and feathers are gasp-worthy and definitely among the most entertaining parts of the show. In The Gilded Age season 4, the fashion is vital to the drama.

#6854. 27 Thoughtful Family Christmas Gifts Everyone Will Be Excited to Unwrap

Every item on this page was hand-picked by a House Beautiful editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

You’re sure to find a winner or two from our carefully curated list.

Buying family Christmas gifts can be more difficult than you think, especially if you’re shopping for an uncle you only see occasionally or the picky cousin who seems to dislike everything. The best place to start is usually to ask those closest to you for ideas, but if the suggestions from others are slow going, you can check out our curated picks ahead.

How did we select them? Well, we asked our editors for the best items they’ve ever received (or given) from their families and kept in mind all the different interests and budgets one might have while looking for gifts.

We found items that appeal to the bird watcher in the family, the sentimentalist, the design lover, and more. The 20+ items range from crowd-pleasers like a heated blanket to hyper-specific items like a stone bathmat for the germaphobes in the family. You can find products across all price points, too, because we know you may want to spend as little as $50 on someone—just know that gifts at this price point still feel thoughtful and look nice—or a couple of hundred on your favorite sibling.

Ahead, shop our top picks for the whole family.

This portable marble lamp only weighs two pounds and adds the most beautiful ambient light to their space. It has three adjustable brightness levels, and the light will gently flash to warn you when the battery is running low. Family members of all ages will appreciate this beauty, from the aunt who wants to display it on her bookshelf to the teenager going off to college who needs to decorate their dorm room to the parent who wants a chic nightlight in their nursery.

If 2027 is their year to get in touch with nature more or pick up a hobby, gift them this smart solar-powered bird feeder to encourage their newfound passion. The bird feeder has both day and nighttime vision, and when paired with a Featherpal subscription, it can identify over 10,000 bird species. #6852. Will There Be Fewer Gowns at the Met Gala Next Year?

Announced today, the theme of the 2027 exhibition explores fashion’s obsession with everyday garments like T-shirts, jeans, and trench coats

Just over two months ago, the Met Costume Institute canceled its John Galliano retrospective, the original theme for the 2027 exhibition, amid donor and patrons outcry—today, it has announced the new direction, and it’s as far from Galliano’s fantastical fashions as possible.

The 2027 Costume exhibition and corresponding gala is titled “extraOrdinary: Fashion and the Poetics of the Everyday.” It will explore how designers reimagine quotidien ideas for the high-fashion world and how wearing, styling, and caring for these garments shapes their significance over time.

The exhibition will be divided into two larger themes. The first, “Familiar Forms,” will center archetypal garments like jeans, T-shirts, biker jackets, and trench coats, looking at how designers reinvent these pieces and play with the symbolism surrounding them. The other, “Common Matter,” will focus on disposable materials like paper, aluminum, and plastic, and how they have been elevated to fashion.

“The ordinary is never quite as simple as it appears,” said Andrew Bolton, Curator in Charge of the Costume Institute. “A pair of jeans, a white shirt, or a sweatshirt can hold traces of a life—the habits, memories, and attachments that make something our own. Designers work with that familiarity, revealing unexpected possibilities within forms and materials we think we already know. The exhibition invites us to discover how much imagination the everyday can hold, and how looking again might change what we value and care for.”

Bolton began where he left off last year with “Costume Art,” and the reality of placing the body at the center of your design process. He was particularly interested in moments like Jun Takahashi’s fall 2024 collection for Undercover, titled “Watching a Working Woman,” which was inspired in part by Wim Wenders’s 2023 film Perfect Days. He created ensembles that joined and layered familiar garments in new ways to subvert and complicate traditional perceptions of identity and form. The exhibition will open with pieces from this collection and expand to include over 200 items, including Matthieu Blazy’s trompe l’oeil leather jeans for Bottega Veneta, pieces from Marc Jacobs’s 1990s Perry Ellis grunge collection, Brooke Shields’s Calvin Klein jeans, Junya Watanabe’s leather biker jackets, Charles James’s 1930s couture puffer coat, and more.

The original theme, which would have centered on Galliano’s life and work, was initially announced in late July. After donors and critics spoke out against the designer’s past antisemitic and anti-Asian remarks, it was then recalled in late August. This decision was unprecedented; the Met had never made such a late-stage switch-up.

Galliano was known for his objectively extraordinary work subverting themes of historical excess and grandeur. His shows were more like theater productions. Now, it seems they’ve chosen a balm for the raucous in skewing in the complete opposite direction, promoting real life over fantasy. That said, this new theme is purported to examine familiar ideas through the extraordinary lens of fashion. There is certainly nothing ordinary about a Junya Watanabe biker jacket.

There is certainly much to explore here. The theme reminds us how much inspiration comes from the people walking down the street every day, the uniforms that have shaped our perceptions of society. Those ideas are ripe material for fashion designers’ creative minds —and can cause quite the stir among audiences. Just think of how shocked the industry was after Marc Jacobs elevated the way youth culture were dressing, what we call “grunge,” to the runway with his 1993 collection for Perry Ellis. Or the fascination with those optical illusion leather jeans Matthieu Blazy put on his Bottega Veneta runways a few years ago. After all, fashion is only fantastical relative to what we deem commonplace. We need a bit of the the familiar to help us digest something radical.

#6850. Under the Surface

Canali gives a behind-the-scenes look at sartorial excellence: the time, precision and mastery that transform raw materials into a jacket made to last

Under the surface, unseen layers lie. They stay there, silent. They don’t demand the spotlight. They don’t seek glory. And yet, like a beating heart, they form the innermost foundation of any real achievement. Where passion, enthusiasm, and discipline meet, the logic of excellence takes shape. Such is the story of Under the Surface, the newest creative collaboration from Canali.

Under the Surface is a look at the unseen architecture of a sartorial jacket by Canali. Every Canali jacket is born from a complex system of about 200 steps, each executed with meticulous care. Around 15 specialists are involved in the process—one that sees more than 520 feet of thread sewn into 22,000 stitches, both machine- and hand-made. Unseen when looking at the finished garment, the process informs each piece’s quality, comfort, shape, and durability.

It’s not just a matter of talent: rather, it’s about staying faithful to one’s goals, pursuing them with dedication even when no one is watching. It’s then that quality truly makes a difference, and the end result becomes the visible manifestation of a long creation process that demands great effort, experience and willpower.

That commitment of excellence unites the sartorial world of Canali and Ethan Hawke, the brand’s new Global Ambassador. The actor, writer, and director has dedicated almost four decades to perfecting his art, with endless, ever-learning curiosity. For their new Fall/Winter 2026 campaign, Canali has decided to capture what goes on behind the scenes. In the film, Hawke pores over his scripts, rehearses lines, refines each motion: a painstaking introspective process made of research, study, and exercise, preceding each of his performances.

The behind-the-scenes of a Canali jacket starts with selecting the finest fabrics, sourced from the best Italian wool mills. Then, each textile is examined and interpreted. While technology now allows extreme precision and control, some assessments are still left to the eye and expertise of human specialists. Even tiny details such as slightly different shades of color or variations in texture can influence the final product.

The fabric is then stabilized via steaming cycles and specific treatments, to ensure its behavior remains consistent over time. Then it’s time for digital mapping, guiding every cutting room decision: here, each of the jacket’s pieces is carefully positioned to also help keep waste to a minimum. It’s the first irreversible step.

The garment’s three-dimensional structure is then shaped upon the canvas. Canali crafts theirs in-house, adapting it to each model’s fit and fabrics. Made from horsehair, plant fibers, and natural reinforcements, the canvas is modeled on custom-made concave shapes, known as Schiena d’Asino (Donkey’s Back), which replicate the chest’s anatomy. The canvas is crucial for determining how a jacket drapes over the body, avoiding stiffness. It’s here that the volume of the chest, the balance of the silhouette and ease of wearing begin to be defined.

Then it’s time to craft the front, lapel, padded shoulder, and the sleeves. From planning to execution, nothing’s left to chance. The tailor observes, corrects, iterates. It’s a patient process, requiring time, knowledge, and skill. Lastly, the various layers are united through basting, stitching, and ironing processes. As these details interact, the garment takes on its structure and sophisticated character.

Something special also takes place behind the scenes with Hawke: day after day, the actor’s preparation subtly transforms his thoughts and behavior until, almost by instinct, he takes on each role’s intimate characteristics.

Time underpins the whole process. After being assembled and pressed, the jacket is allowed to rest, to dissipate the steam and stabilize the cloth. This is how the fabric finds its balance, and the garment gains a sort of memory: the ability to naturally find its shape again, even after having been worn, folded, or packed into a suitcase.

In over 90 years of heritage, Canali has refined their techniques through repetition, experience, and experimentation. Each garment is built upon the time-tested values of care, know-how, precision. The Canali style is not one made of grand gestures, but daily practice, intimate and subtle: the constant research into a shape that lasts. One hero of the Under the Surface project is the vicuña jacket, available exclusively at selected flagship boutiques by Canali.The campaign, and the collaboration between Canali and Ethan Hawke, speak to the art of shaping oneself, guided by an ambition to do things well with patience and self-awareness. Both in tailoring and in cinema, there is so much quiet work that happens behind the scenes to make creativity possible. Discipline and rigour do not constrain creative freedom; they make it possible. For Canali, excellence takes shape through the craftsmanship with which each element is made. For Ethan Hawke, it is the preparation that allows him to approach each role with confidence.And, as the Under The Surface campaign reveals, each of these steps adds something essential to the finished product. In the case of the Canali cashmere and vicuña blazer (the hero style of the FW26 campaign), it is structure, movement, character, durability, and a lightness that can only be achieved through the combination of expertly-chosen materials and complex fine tailoring. The knowledge required to execute this is almost tangible, felt in the quality of the piece.

The finished result only has value because of the strong, unseen structure underneath. It is a story we can all resonate with. Little by little, layer by layer, day by day, we each build ourselves in pursuit of excellence. Our actions create the people we are – even, or especially, when nobody is watching – in the same way that the apparent simplicity of a piece of clothing hides the depth of work required to craft it. This is the essence of Under the Surface.

#6847. How Club Kid Fashion Went From the Dance Floor to the Runway

The underground scene’s DIY, gender-bending style still shapes how we get dressed today.

For his fall/winter 2009 runway show, “The Horn of Plenty,” Alexander McQueen offered a satirical critique of fashion’s throwaway culture. Models navigated a runway strewn with glass and discarded props in houndstooth looks and silhouettes that mocked fashion’s excess. Their alabaster-white painted faces and exaggerated lips evoked Leigh Bowery, the Australian performance artist known for his audacious club looks. “It’s a sackable offense, this collection,” McQueen said post-show. “I want people to look at it and say, ‘What the hell is that?’”

Much like McQueen upended fashion with his confrontational approach to design, Club Kids—a name given to the scene’s most flamboyant clubgoers, Bowery included—did the same in cities like New York and London. The scene was rife with larger-than-life, sometimes debaucherous personalities expressing themselves through equally outsized clothes; no look was too outlandish, no party too wild. It was that DIY spirit, championed by the likes of Susanne Bartsch, a nightlife impresario, RuPaul, and the infamous Michael Alig, that caught on beyond the clubs, creating an inextricable connection between nightlife and fashion. “You didn’t have to wait for a designer or a magazine to tell you what was fashionable,” Bartsch tells ELLE. “You could create a look, walk into the room, and see what happened. The crowd became the show, and the dance floor became a runway with no fixed rules.”

“Susanne, when she moved to New York, she opened a boutique and was the first person to carry clothes by [John] Galliano and [Jean Paul] Gaultier, [really] introducing that type of style into the city,” explains Ryan Burke, a New York-based makeup artist. “And then she also threw these parties; it went really hand in hand.”

Bartsch’s legendary parties at Savage and the Copacabana brought together a true amalgamation of worlds, from downtown denizens to fashion heavyweights like Thierry Mugler and Gaultier. (Like McQueen, Gaultier referenced Bowery in a 1991 runway show.) It was not only an intermixing of punchy, confident aesthetics, but also the idea that fashion can be an escape and the ultimate form of self-expression. My club nights in the ’80s brought together artists, designers, photographers, performers, and kids creating their own looks,” Bartsch notes. “For someone who has felt judged or excluded, that can be powerful. The [dancefloor] gives you permission to try something on not just an outfit, but a version of yourself.”

Meanwhile, in London, the Blitz Kids channeled that same appetite for theatrical self-expression through their DIY, gender-bending looks, which would influence collections by Marc Jacobs (most famously, his grunge collection for Perry Ellis), McQueen, and Anna Sui.

The AIDS crisis took an enormous toll on the communities that made New York nightlife pulse. Then came New York’s tough-on-crime crackdown, which helped drive many of the most popular clubs out of business. Today, the Club Kids’ heyday has the glow of downtown legend: a moment when self-expression ruled the dance floor and anyone could make an entrance. Now, Burke notes that social media has changed how people create and share their looks, making the heyday of the Club Kids feel all the more singular. (In film, the scene’s legacy has been in 2003’s Chloë Sevigny-starring Party Monster and is now slyly resurfacing in Jordan Firstman’s new film, Club Kid, where he plays a club promoter whose life is upended by the arrival of the 10-year-old son he never knew he had.)

“People are really crafty, really crafty online; they create, document it, take it off,” he says. “That’s cool too; any kind of way people want to express themselves is great, but there was something that was really magical about seeing people out. They tried more interesting things that might not work or might not look that good, but they tried it.”

The Club Kid spirit still reverberates in the underground scenes of New York, London, and beyond—and at the highest levels of fashion. You just need to know where to look.

During the recent spring/summer 2027 season, when many designers celebrated the “joy of getting dressed,” the club was never too far away. At Junya Watanabe, pearls and punk hardware were stacked atop catsuits with the carefree abandon of the original club kids, who built their looks from punk boutiques and thrift-store finds. Diesel got hot and heavy: Glenn Martens’s swan song for the brand unfolded amid a crowd of performers kissing and touching, while models wore barely-there denim and clothes that looked hastily thrown on, finished with smudged red lipstick, as if they’d been smooching on the dance floor. And August Barron showed a starlet-inspired lineup of glitzy “going out” dresses, sandals with neon heels, and haphazardly layered separates.

It’s easy to see why fashion keeps returning to the club: Nowhere else makes getting dressed feel quite so alive. As Bartsch puts it: “For me, the club spirit isn’t one particular look, it’s freedom, imagination and the confidence to express yourself without asking permission.”

#6846. Is Black Doves Returning for Season 3?

The Keira Knightley–led spy thriller finally returns to Netflix with a cliff-hanger that demands more episodes.

This story contains spoilers for the season 2 finale of Black Doves.

Black Doves was immediately one of the best spy thrillers on Netflix when it premiered two years ago. Unlike The Night Agent’s stilted acting and Reacher’s nonstop machismo, the British drama featured stellar acting from Keira Knightley in her most prominent role since her last Pirates of the Caribbean film. But after a year passed by without any new episodes or updates, I figured the spy series would never return.

Luckily, Netflix eventually gave Black Doves the green light and season 2 debuted in full on the streamer on Thursday, November 5. Alongside Ben Whishaw as hitman Sam Young, Knightley returns as Helen Webb, a global spy for hire who sells information to the highest bidder. She also happens to be the UK prime minister’s wife—which is not ideal when you’re trying to keep secrets.

In the first season, Helen also had to manage an affair that turned sour when her lover, Jason, was murdered. He was later revealed to be an MI5 agent sent to investigate her—you really can’t trust anyone if you’re a spy—but solving his murder was just the start of Helen’s problems. By the season 1 finale, audiences learned that the nanny who takes care of her children is also a spy.

The plot continues in season 2, as Helen learns how to navigate her shady job and her responsibilities as the prime minister’s wife. We also learn more about Reed (Sarah Lancashire), who works as Helen’s handler in the Black Doves spy organization. It turns out that her daughter is Helen’s nanny. Meanwhile, a new criminal outfit named Kestral plans to assassinate the prime minister and blame the killing on Helen and the Doves.

But at the end of season 2, Helen is forced to reveal her double life to her husband, who is about to find out just how much information he’s unknowingly passed on to her buyers over their years together. However, his response will likely set up season 3, as the cliff-hanger leaves audiences right before Helen begins telling him the truth about their relationship.

As of now, Netflix has yet to renew the spy thriller for a third season. That’s only natural. The second season just aired on the streamer. But according to creator Joe Barton, the writers room is already putting together potential ideas for season 3.

“Netflix are really behind it,” he told The Sun earlier this week. “They’ve said that thing, ‘We’d love to recommission it’—I’m like, No one’s stopping you, you can!”

Hopefully, another two years won’t pass before we find our answer.

#6837. Megan Skiendel of KATSEYE Reveals the Real Reason for the Recent String of Concert Cancellations

The group postponed three shows after previously citing Megan’s illness.

It’s no secret that KATSEYE’s Wildworld Tour has been...a bit rocky, to say the least. With two of its members—Sophia Laforteza and Manon Bannerman—taking extended breaks from performing with the global girl group, the four remaining members (Daniela Avanzini, Lara Raj, Megan Skiendel, and Yoonchae) kicked off the tour’s North American leg on October 13, only to end up canceling their shows in Detroit, Minneapolis, and—most recently—Chicago.

The first cancellation occurred on November 3, less than an hour before the group was set to perform at Little Caesars Arena in Detroit. At the time, the group released an Instagram Story apology, saying the show was “postponed due to illness” and that “Megan is unwell and unable to perform.”

Two days later, on November 5, the official Instagram pages for Chicago’s United Center and Minneapolis’ Target Center shared statements announcing the postponements, once again citing Megan’s illness.

Not long after, the official KATSEYE Instagram account released another Story statement, this time explaining what was really going on.

“The label has made the decision to postpone our shows in Chicago and Minneapolis as Megan is recovering from a concussion,” the group wrote, adding that they would “announce the rescheduled dates as soon as possible.”

Soon after, Megan took to her own Stories to release a statement, telling fans, “I currently have a concussion and am doing everything I possibly can to recover from it so I can be on stage for you all again.”

She added that she’s been “strongly advised by medical professionals that I need to take this time to fully recover and come back safely.”

Quick reminder that this ^^^ all comes after KATSEYE was forced to pull out of Olivia Rodrigo’s Daisy Chain Fields festival back in August after Megan sprained her ankle during tour rehearsal. Per the band, Megan was evaluated by doctors and told to stay off her ankle and allow it to properly heal ahead of the group’s upcoming tour.

All this to say: Can we all agree that these women need (and deserve) an extended break??

#6836. An Everyman’s Guide to Wearing a Necklace

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

You already have a watch you like. This is the next step in your jewelry journey.

Men’s jewelry, I’ve learned, can be a slippery slope. I’ve been a watch guy since elementary school, if we’re generous enough to count a dual-sided compass/watch on a carabiner as a watch. In college I added a bracelet. I’ve been a ring guy for over ten years, thanks mostly to being married and partially to getting lunch drunk in Albuquerque, going ring shopping for my wife, and finding a sterling silver bear signet ring that I’ve worn nonstop since. I’ve had an earring for two years, thanks to a quick weekend in Miami and a well-timed Harrison Ford interview clip crossing my algorithm about getting his ear pierced in a Claire’s.

Recently, I’ve been curious about my personal final frontier of men’s jewelry—the necklace. The other listings above are all fairly low entry points, safe experiments in the world of men’s jewelry. The earring may be the exception, but this is 2026; one small silver hoop isn’t that daring, even in the Midwest. A necklace has never really been my thing. I haven’t felt the pull. That is, until this year.

For an unrelated project, I was breaking down some of the signature stylistic calling cards of Robert Redford. And there it was, across paparazzi pictures and movie stills and in memoriam roundups. In almost every photo, Redford has some kind of necklace, usually peeking from a precariously unbuttoned western shirt.

Further study of what we can safely call Hollywood’s golden age of off-duty style shows the same: McQueen and Newman and their contemporaries, swanning around on and off film, always wearing some sort of necklace or chain or pendant.

This is, of course, a common trap: to look at a famously handsome Hollywood star and think, yeah, all that’s separating me from THAT is a necklace. And sure, a good cheekbone and clavicle will get you pretty far in life. There’s no magic amulet here, nothing that will shazam you into leading-man looks. But there are a few lessons on styling from guys whose literal job it was to present themselves favorably to the world. And a few lessons on what necklaces to choose to help get there.

So, I put my professional online shopping style writing gig into overdrive, going deep into the Getty Images archives and confusing Google’s settings by repeatedly searching “Insert Hollywood Icon + shirtless” to glean some jewelry 101 tips from the professionals.

Instagram and (theoretically, since I don’t indulge) TikTok are full of maximalizing style trend chasers. Why wear one necklace when you can wear four? Why two rings when each finger could have two rings on it? Why dip your toes in the water when you can cannonball into the deep end? And sure, there’s some je ne sais quoi about just committing to something fully and completely out of the gate. But there’s also a lot of try-hard to it.

We’re channeling a more reserved, less #sponsoredpost era of dressing here that cares less about link clicks and more about how things look in the real world. So start simple and start with a chain. My favorite, and currently on daily rotation, is the Curb Chain from L.A.-based Bloedstone. Mine is sterling silver and in the inch length, which hits closer to the sternum than the neck. That’s the prescribed Hollywood A-lister way to wear it, but chart your own path if you want to. Miansai has a thicker 2mm Cuban chain that’s great too. And while we’re covering the M’s, Mejuri has one too. To my slight surprise, my home state watchmaker Shinola makes one, and less surprisingly so, Esquire-approved David Yurman has this giant one featuring a marketing campaign with Michael B. Jordan, who is picking up McQueen’s (and subsequently Brosnan’s) torch by adapting The Thomas Crown Affair.

There’s a whole vocabulary on link types and thickness and clasps out there. Don’t worry about that for now. Find something that calls to you and click add to cart. We’re starting slowly here.

Parting note. There are a lot of chains worn over T-shirts these days. To each their own, but the leading man’s move is to keep it under your shirt. If you want to show it off, just ditch the buttons.

A chain is simple, and sure, the greats wore them. But more often than not, they had something dangling from that chain. Medallion, pendant, a bottle opener in Cool Hand Luke. McQueen was often photographed, including in the pages of Esquire, wearing a St. Christopher medallion. Seeing as he’s the patron saint of travelers, St. Christopher medallions are having a bit of a cultural resurgence, partially because of their traditional association as a good-luck charm for travelers. There are some fun enamel ones on eBay, including these surfing-specific ones. Every once in a while, Nashville-based Americana experts Imogene + Willie will pull together a vintage edit of St. Christopher medallions that always sell out in around 24 hours. Speaking of Nashville, if you’re going to go new, go with Yearly Co. The gold jewelry experts have a 14k version of the iconography with a pitch-perfect 24-inch chain. I can attest to the quality—I gave my wife the brand’s diamond huggies and she wears them almost every day. As the grandson of a Protestant minister, I steer away from the saints out of genetic loyalty to Luther’s 95 Theses, but I recently picked up this Perseverance medallion from the brand, inspired by a vintage signet ring. It scratches the McQueen itch with flying colors.

Secondhand gold is also an option. Alloy Market recently came onto my radar, and I’m browsing it daily for myself and gifts for my wife. They’ve got a small but mighty collection of certified men’s items with pendants and medallions you can mix and match. All of the quality without any of the markup.

Don’t limit yourself to just a medallion, though. Get a little funky. Redford did for a while with this turquoise number that somehow looked even cooler hitting under the sternum. Shinola’s got an option there too. Bali-based Salty Dagger has an Odysseus-approved Greek mythology piece. And why not try a locket, like this one from Pattaraphan. Miansai has a great pendant. Get fancy with the Prevaut diamond ring deal. When in doubt, steal from the girls. I got this With Clarity birthstone necklace for my wife that I might take a swing at myself.

Peyote Bird High Desert Sterling Silver Necklace

Shinola 21mm Turquoise Inlay Tag Necklace

From the abbreviated/slightly bastardized Shakespeare: “All the world’s a stage and one man in his time plays many parts.” Jewelry can feel like a risk, even if you’re following some sort of contrived guide to styling and buying it. Sometimes we pretend until we’re comfortable actually being. Redford and McQueen and company were actors. Pretending was their legacy, and they were good at it. Take a leaf from their book and get dressed for the curtain call.

#6834. I Found the Stocking Stuffers Your Beauty-Obsessed Friend Will Actually Use

Including easy-to-divide gift sets for extra savings.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

Big-ticket beauty gifts like a Dyson hair tool or an LED mask may get all the attention during the holidays, but some of the most coveted presents come in small packages—aka stocking stuffers. As a beauty editor, my favorite gifts to give (and receive...hint, hint) are the little treasures I can toss in my purse and reach for all day. Consider this your expert guide to the best beauty stocking stuffers to stock up on this holiday season.

Think of this list as a mini beauty haul, with plenty of editor-backed goodies they’ll actually use, from pimple patch restocks and luxurious hand creams to travel-friendly picks that can make seat 34F feel like first class. Pro tip: I also included gift sets you can split up to fill multiple stockings and save a few bucks. Most picks are under $50, but I also threw in a few luxe finds for anyone you’re ready to spoil.

Medicube’s latest viral hit is basically made for a stocking. This easy-to-swipe balm stick pairs PDRN, collagen, caffeine, vitamin E, and Volufiline to hydrate and give skin a plumper-looking finish wherever they want it, from under the eyes to smile lines. It’s a fun upgrade to their usual eye patch routine—and one they can use on the go.

Our editors—along with the rest of the internet—got hooked on Eos’ Vanilla Cashmere line. The Shea Better body lotion gives winter skin a serious hit of hydration without feeling heavy, and the scent is so good. While the classic body lotion is always an old faithful, why not gift them something with a little extra glam like this holiday edition shimmery version? It’s the same nourishing formula, but with a golden, pearlescent finish for glowy skin.

Another useful beauty stocking stuffer? Dae’s mascara-inspired finishing wand will tame flyaways and frizz for a polished look. The styling cream gives hair flexible hold without leaving it crunchy or stiff. Plus, it’s convenient to carry in a handbag so they can touch up whenever, wherever.

I’ll never say no to more hydrating lippies—and the scents on my personal wish list? Summer Fridays’ vanilla beige, hot cocoa, and brown sugar. Formulated with shea butter, murumuru seed butter, and hyaluronic acid, this viral lip balm leaves a glassy finish while imparting a buildable tint. Plus, we’ve found that despite its compact size, it lasts for months.

These hydrogel masks are one of the most viral products for achieving an overnight glow—and our editors can confirm they’re worth the obsession. Ingredients like hyaluronic acid, niacinamide, and collagen infuse your skin with hydration and brighten for that glass skin effect. Beauty editor Beth Gillette says it leaves her with “the smoothest skin ever.”

You can never have too many claw clips—so they’re sure to appreciate this stylish four-pack that comes in soothing colors and bedazzled designs. They are available in a range of colors and patterns, so pick a few that match their aesthetic.

If they’rea already a fan of Charlotte Tilbury’s Pillow Talk line, give them this Plumpgasm lip gloss. Formulated with hyaluronic acid and hydrolyzed collagen, the high shine gloss leaves my lips feeling deeply hydrated and super plumped. The color payoff is strong enough that I can skip lipstick and wear it with just a stain or lip liner underneath.

Stuff their stocking with a real winter essential: hand cream. L’Occitane’s Holiday Hand Cream Trio comes with three 1-ounce tubes that can live in their bag. Made from shea butter, the formula delivers long-lasting hydration without feeling too thick and slippery. The nourishing hand creams come in three festive scents: original shea, praline, and pine.

A set of fun press-on nails will always come in handy when they need an emergency mani. Get them this gorgeous cat-eye style that’s perfect for New Year’s Eve, or snag them other sets Glamnetic sells. Our editors can attest that they last for weeks when applied correctly, so you’re basically gifting them a free salon-level manicure.

When winter has me dreaming of warmer days, Sol de Janeiro’s tropical-scented bodycare set gives my shower routine a mini vacation. With notes of vanilla orchid and sheer sandalwood, this travel-size set includes Delícia Drench Shower Oil for leaving skin silky-soft post-shower, the rich Delícia Drench Body Butter, which helps lock in moisture, and the Cheirosa 59 Perfume Mist to match.

A Cosmo Holy Grail Beauty Award winner, Cyklar’s Perfume Oil is the perfect purse-sized pick for a quick scent refresh on the go. “The scent is so warm, rich, and creamy without being sickly sweet or smelling artificial,” says senior director of editorial operations, Danielle Colin-Thome. “I couldn’t wait to finish testing the other perfume oils on my list so I could go back to using this every day.”

Perfect for the jet-setter in your life, this little set of skincare patches will help revive their complexion, especially after a long travel day. It comes with two hydrating sheet masks infused with hyaluronic acid and vitamin B5, two pairs of under-eye patches (one for daytime and one for nighttime), and a lip mask.

If a Rhode Pocket Blush showed up in my stocking, I’d be doing a very happy little dance. So if you’re shopping for a fellow Rhode fan, it’s never a bad idea to restock their favorite shade. This blush is famous for its creamy formula that’s effortless to blend out for a natural-looking flush.

Winter dryness has me reaching for this hydrating mist on repeat, making it a useful addition to their stocking. It’s formulated with PDRN oil capsules, hyaluronic acid, and hydrolyzed collagen that leaves a light, even layer of moisture in just a few spritzes. My skin looks naturally dewy with or without makeup.

Between the delicious scents and plush, whipped texture, this body butter has our editors hooked.

“There’s something magical about this body butter...It beautifully melts into skin and feels weightless,” says contributor Mary Honkus. “The thickness is just right, and the scents are to die for.” It comes with three travel-size pouches so you can tuck one into a stocking or split them among your besties.

Okay, okay. The price on this one isn’t *exactly* stocking stuffer-friendly—but for the skincare-obsessed person on your list, the Depuffi is a little luxury that is totally worth the splurge. It offers hot and cool therapy with six temperature settings to help soothe facial tension and de-puff. “The shape fits the face perfectly, really getting in on all the contours and curves of my cheeks and jawline,” says assistant beauty editor Jasmine Hyman in her review. “The cooling setting is perfect to close out the facial by reducing post-suction redness and providing some extra depuffing.”

A Cosmo Readers’ Choice Award winner, Salt & Stone’s Body Wash Discovery Set is another easy gift to split among a few stockings. The luxe scents in this set—like saffron and cedar or bergamot and hinoki—smell way more elevated than their boring body washes. Or gift the full set to the friend who treats a long, steamy shower like their favorite way to unwind.

A girl can never have too many hair ties, and this set of mini silk scrunchies from Squigs is a useful stocking stuffer that will surely be appreciated. They have a soft, secure hold that will keep your hair out of the way without creasing or tugging. A little birdie told us that Gracie Abrams uses them too.

Hand sanitizer is a cold and flu season essential, but Gemi’s formula feels a touch more elevated than the usual alcohol-scented, ultra-drying kind. It comes in luxe scents like lavender and geranium, green tea and bergamot, and Fuji apple and rose, and it’s formulated with cica, vitamin B5, and green tea to help keep hands feeling hydrated. Get the version with an attached keychain, and it doubles as a cute bag charm.

When my skin needs a reset, I reach for Dr. Dennis Gross Skincare’s Alpha Beta Universal Daily Peel Pads. The two-step treatment helps smooth texture, brighten my complexion, and fade the look of dark spots over time. This set of five is the perfect little pick-me-up for dull winter skin, especially when my skin is craving a deeper exfoliation.

#6827. Here’s What It Takes to Keep a Pool Running During the Winter, According to Experts

Before you dive in, it’s important to understand the risks and expenses involved.

When most people think of backyard swimming pools, relaxing outdoors in the summertime immediately comes to mind. But some homeowners don’t want the swimming season to end when temperatures drop. Fans of cold-water swimming are starting to consider ways to enjoy their pools throughout the winter as a part of an at-home wellness experience.

“Ten years ago, most people didn’t think winter swims were possible, especially in colder climates,” says Karen Larson, cofounder of Soake Pools. “Times have changed because these days, they’re seeing neighbors, friends, and others do it and post the proof on social media.”

The increasing interest in cold-water experiences is part of a broader wellness trend. In American College of Sports Medicine's 2026 Worldwide Fitness Trends report, cold therapies, including cold plunges, cryotherapy, and cold showers, earned the 17th spot among fitness trends for the year, up from number 20 in its 2025 report.

If you’ve previously closed your swimming pool around Labor Day but are interested in making cold-water swimming a part of your daily wellness routine, there’s a lot to keep in mind. Not every existing pool is a good candidate for keeping open for winter swimming, and it’s important to understand the risks and expenses involved. But if you’re curious, here’s what to consider about keeping a pool open year-round.

Why is cold-water swimming becoming more common? There are numerous purported mental and wellness benefits, from athletic recovery to stress relief and better sleep. A 2025 systematic review and meta-analysis published in PLOS ONE found that cold-water immersion led to a reduction in stress for 12 hours afterward as well as improvements in sleep quality and overall quality of life.

“Winter swimming often creates a time where friends and family can come together without distractions,” adds Larson.

That said, while swimming during the colder months has its perks, there are also several risks to keep in mind. A 2020 review in the International Journal of Environmental Research and Public Health found that cold-water immersion can trigger an immediate shock response, leading to involuntary gasping, hyperventilation, and a rapid increase in heart rate and blood pressure. These reactions can make it difficult to control your breathing and increase your risk of drowning. Cold temps can also impair your muscle strength and nerve function, making it challenging to swim safely.

It’s a good idea to acclimate to the cold water gradually and avoid prolonged exposure. You’ll also want to make sure there’s someone else home while you take your cold-water plunge in the pool.

In addition, if you have underlying conditions like heart disease, consult your doctor before all else, as the cold water may place additional strain on your heart.

If you’re trying to consider whether your pool is a good candidate to keep open during the colder months, these are the things to look into.

The cost of keeping a swimming pool open for winter swimming will depend on your location, size, desired water temperature, and the type of heater you use.

“Routine pool maintenance generally remains about the same year-round, although chemical consumption may decrease depending on how frequently the pool is used,” explains Jim Eisch, Jr., vice president of operations at Pinch A Penny Pool Patio Spa. “The biggest expense to consider in the winter is heating the pool.”

For this reason, prospective winter swimmers may want to consider an upgrade to their heating system. Instead of using pricey natural gas heating or a traditional heat pump, Eisch says that inverter technology is one of the most affordable ways to maintain a swimmable pool temperature year-round.

For example, if you live in Florida and have a 10,000-gallon swimming pool, Eisch estimates your annual heating costs will be $2,467 with a natural gas heater, $741 with a traditional heat pump, and $434 for a heat/cool pump that uses inverter technology.

As aforementioned, the goal with wintertime swimming is not to be soaking in bathwater-like temperatures while it’s freezing outside. For safety purposes, winter swims are quick, and the heating system is to keep your chlorinated pool water well above the 19-degree Fahrenheit freezing point.

Unsurprisingly, a standard 14-by-28-foot swimming pool is going to be one of the hardest to use effectively year-round, unless you live in a region with a mild wintertime climate.

“You can technically use a large pool year-round, but it’s difficult to cover all its surface area well enough to hold in the heat, so heating bills can climb fast,” says Larson.

Therefore, smaller sizes like a 10-by-16-foot cocktail pool or plunge pool are better candidates for keeping open. Dedicated winter swimmers should also invest in an easy on-and-off cover—or a motorized auto cover—for added protection.

Regular pool cleaning and maintenance are essential, and this upkeep over the winter is no exception.

“Check the filtration system regularly to make sure it’s circulating smoothly, monitor the water chemistry, and plan for power outages—because there’s a good chance they will happen,” Larson explains. For this reason, you should also invest in a generator that can keep the pump running if the power goes out.

Safe pool access is just as important as any other aspect of the pool’s design. That’s why a nonslip surface, excellent drainage, and ample lighting are key.

“Your design should also provide a clear path from the house to the water,” says Neha Gupta, interior designer and founder at NU Curations.

Especially in the wintertime when ice and snow are a factor in your region, make sure you have a safe and smooth exit plan for getting out of the water, bundled up, and into your warm home without any obstacles. And then, of course, remember to cover the pool back up to keep it protected and ready for your next plunge.

#6823. 10 Stylish Sofa Covers to Totally Refresh Your Living Room Before the Holidays

Every item on this page was hand-picked by a House Beautiful editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Hide stains, protect upholstery, and add instant polish.

Life at home gets messy with its regular residents, and it gets even crazier when guests come over. Hosting season demands the most of our couches, as they become extra seats for family dinner or makeshift beds for overnight stays. It’s not always feasible to swap out your sofa just because the upholstery has seen better days. That’s where the mighty sofa cover comes in.

Whether you opt for a tightly-fitted silhouette or a looser, blanket-style slipcover, a high-quality sofa cover can totally refresh your space. Just think about it: a dated, yellowing couch suddenly looks fresh and brand new. You can even go bold and experiment with patterned options, without the commitment of buying a whole new piece. Pro tip: patterns are also a great way to hide stains.

There are hundreds of sofa cover options available, so below, our shopping team did the hard part for you. We hunted down nine options for different types of couches, including an editor-tested and approved option. Plus, we chatted with interior designer Tiffany Leigh for tips on styling sofa covers, keeping them clean, and more.

Velvet couches are having a moment, so if you want in on the trend but don't want to toss out your current sofa, enter this sumptuous sofa slipcover. Stretch the base piece to fit around a sofa measuring 72 to 96 inches, then cover three cushions with the included covers. The fabric is extra soft, so you feel like you're sitting on a high-end sofa at a fraction of the price. "My family used this velvet slipcover to bring a dated, faded blue sofa back to life in a trendy ivory velvet that looked like it could be at home in any furniture showroom," Associate Shopping Editor Janae McKenzie says. "Even better: it was so soft against my skin that I fell asleep on it during movie night more times than I'm proud of."

Material: Velvet | Colors: 23 | Care: Machine washable

Full-blown tassels might feel like too much texture to add to your everyday sofa, but some subtle fringe is a welcome inclusion. This blanket-style cover drapes easily over a two-three cushion couch, and it’s made of a non-slip fabric designed to stay in place. Even with the rowdiest kids and pets.

Material: Chenille | Colors: 18 | Care: Machine washable

Just because you want to protect your couch doesn’t mean you have to give up dreams of elegant white furniture. This extra-large cream couch cover actually comes in two pieces, so it’s perfect to cover an L-Shaped or U-Shaped sectional. You can protect the chaise arm with ease, and it’s still stain-resistant and machine-washable.

Material: Basketweave, Linen, Boucle, Velvet, and more | Colors: 95 | Care: Depends on the fabric material you chose

The size of your sofa cover will depend not only on the size of the sofa or couch you want to protect, but also on the properties of the fabric itself. Leigh says that with a stretchy fabric, you may want a cover with measurements slightly smaller than the dimensions of your sofa for a tighter, more fitted look. For sofa covers with corner ties that allow you to tighten or tie bows, the fabric may have a bit less give, so you'll want to allow for a little extra room in the cover size.

Some styles lend themselves to being covered more effectively than others. Leigh says that sofas with thinner or rolled arms tend to look nicer with a slipcover than those without. You can also choose between having a skirt on your sofa cover or showing off the legs of your furniture, depending on your aesthetic preference. “I like when they go right to the floor because a skirt cover is a much more traditional, covered look,” Leigh says. “If you have a little bit more of a modern style, having a slipcover that stops at the legs can be a fresher, kind of more updated take.”

Natural fabrics, such as chenille or linen, are typically a great choice when selecting a sofa cover fabric. For family rooms or homes with pets, indoor/outdoor fabrics like Crypton that have been treated to repel dirt and debris are safer bets for those wanting something stain-resistant. The stretchiness of the fabric also plays a role in selecting a sofa cover. "If you wanted something a little bit more durable, canvas or cotton would be a really great material on there," Leigh says.

It's always a good idea to pay attention to the care instructions for whatever cover you buy, as fabric care really depends on the individual material. For example, a heavier cotton material can likely withstand more washing. “I would say only wash when you really need to, just like with a really nice sweater,” Leigh says. “Don't wash it every time you wear it because washing breaks down fibers. So, it could reduce the longevity of your sofa cover. Most are designed to be removed and washed. So if and when you feel like 'oh, you know what, it's looking a little bit dingy, it's time,' that's the time to do it.” Additionally, it's essential to consider the size of your cover, as larger ones may necessitate the use of a commercial washing machine.

Additionally, the cover may wrinkle over time, especially with linen, which softens with each wash. “Linen does get a little bit wrinkly, but I think that's kind of the charm of a slipcovered sofa, having that kind of rumpled, you know, 'someone just took a nap here' look,” Leigh says.

“An oatmeal linen slipcover is that very quintessential, relaxed coastal style that you see a lot of slipcovers used in, and I think it's a classic choice that you'll never go wrong with,” Leigh says. Don't run away from patterns either, though—Leigh is seeing a rise in patterned upholstery in the design industry, and it's a chance to push the envelope and have fun with your design choices. “A cover can be a great way to, you know, try out a pattern without necessarily committing to it forever, because if in five years you say, ‘okay, this pattern was fun, but I'm ready for something different,’ you can just change the slipcover as opposed to having to get an all-new sofa,” Leigh says.

At House Beautiful, we are experts in reviewing and buying anything for the home, from mattresses for back pain to reclining sofas. For this story in particular, we asked our staff members to share their favorite sofa cover (if they own one) and spent hours combing the internet, reading hundreds of reviews about the best ones currently available on the market. While we haven't personally tested every product on this list, we've thoroughly outlined the pros and cons of each sofa cover to provide you with all the necessary details before you make a purchase.

For further insight into how to best place sofa covers in your home, we consulted interior designer Tiffany Leigh. Leigh runs her eponymous firm in Ontario, Canada, where her team helps clients bring their dream homes and cottages to life. Since 2019, they have designed approachable and livable interiors that feel welcoming and elevated, focusing on quality craftsmanship, emotional connection, and natural elements.

#6816. 7 Desert Boots That Can Survive the Winter

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

Despite the name, these chukkas are built to be worn in all conditions.

Winter weather can get ugly. If it’s not wind and rain, it’s slushy sidewalks and unexpected snow flurries. You obviously need shoes that can handle a little precipitation, but the key phrase here is a little. You don’t want to stomp around in heavy winter boots, especially if we want to look nice. Dress shoes are a tough fit in winter, as are dress boots. Funny enough, between December and March, you know what our go-to footwear becomes? Desert boots. Technically chukka boots, but a few desert boots make the cut here.

But that’s something to note. The Esquire office does not use the term interchangeably—but the Internet does, so we’re going along with it. We know some of you—nerds, mostly—have stopped reading this article because you don’t think we know the difference between the two. We’re well aware of the square and rectangle-ism: All desert boots are chukkas, not all chukkas are desert boots.

If you’re not a nerd and want to learn something new, chukkas are ankle-high boots with open lacing and two or three pairs of eyelets, often made with leather uppers—leather or rubber soled. Desert boots are a subsection of this silhouette that typically features a suede upper (nubuck, sometimes) with lighter-colored, more flexible crepe soles. They are not as winterized—suede, especially, is known to take a beating from moisture—hence the name. The details vary here and there, but both styles can work for winter if you choose wisely.

We found seven pairs of chukka boots—yes, some of which are desert boots—with weather-friendly features, from water-resistant uppers to sturdy rubber soles. Most have both, so you can find a pair that looks sharp without leaving you entirely at the mercy of the winter weather forecast.

These chukka boots might as well be dress boots. They look sharp no matter what outfit you pair them with, and the ultra-refined, premium calfskin leather upper is naturally water-resistant; to a point, that is—we wouldn’t wear them in the pouring rain.

They’re very comfortable thanks to a soft, leather lining on the inside and open-cell foam insoles that provide supportive cushioning. And the flexible outsoles made from XL Extralight injection molding are a nice touch that give the boot some lightweight flexibility.

For a classic pair of chukka boots that come at a good price and have a wide range of colors and sizes, Thursday Boot Co. is the way to go. These chukkas come in a variety of colors and materials, but our favorite has to be the Mocha colorway in weather-safe suede. It’s rare to find a pair of suede shoes you can actually get wet without ruining, but these ones can brave the weather while still looking great. And with studded rubber outsoles, you have some extra traction and durability. If you want even more traction and durability in the soles, try one of the Rugged & Resilient styles with anti-slip wedge rubber outsoles.

Here’s another suede option you can actually wear during the winter; Sabah treats the suede upper of this boot to be water resistant, but you can still add a suede treatment for extra protection if you want to. These boots are handmade in Monte San Giusto, Italy, and you can trust that they were made with lots of skill and care. The insole and midsole are made from vegetable tanned leather, so they end up forming to your feet once you wear them, making them extra comfortable.

If you like the look of a gum-sole sneaker, you might like how it looks on a boot, too. With these desert boots, SeaVees blends the classic gum-sole look with the more rugged desert boot style, and the result is a versatile boot that can withstand pretty much any weather. With water-resistant rugged oiled leather uppers and natural rubber soles, these guys can get you through quite a lot.

These are going to be the least weather-ready on this list. You don’t want to take them out in a snowstorm, but damn do they look good. Imagine these worn to a holiday party, worn with a nice pair of chinos and fair-isle sweater.

The suede upper is a true not-water-resistant suede. But they do have durable rubber soles. The stitched moc-toe is a fun detail that makes these chukka boots stand out from the rest of the pack. Drake’s notes that these boots run small, so they recommend sizing up by a half-size when ordering online.

We’re starting to get pretty far away from the chukka heart, but you can see the chukka-shaped vamp and the ankle-y shape. Plus, when we think of rugged workwear boots, we think of Timberland. The upper is made from waterproof leather and has a TimberDry waterproof membrane for extra protection, and you can see that Gripstick rubber outsole gives you some distance from the ground and provides great traction as well.

And finally, we can’t talk about chukka boots without mentioning Clarks. The original Clarks desert boot was designed in 1949 and inspired by the rough suede and crepe-soled boots worn by off-duty British soldiers during WWII (as well as the shoes worn by polo players in India and South African veldskoen shoes). Today, the brand’s original style is still going strong, and you can get a pair for under $100.
